ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u24	All right. Amendment No 840 is intended to clarify that the measurement of the health pass must be limited in time and space, and to be based, I would stress, on the local impact, contagiosity and pathogenicity of the circulating SARS-CoV-2 variant(s) and on local vaccination coverage. Minister, in the communication from the Government, the signals are alarmist about the contagion, and I see, moreover, the same figures as you, but we are not given any explanations, or only very little, about the pathogenicity of the circulating variant and the other variant that is coming. However, it is important to know whether the symptoms and physiopathology of this disease are changing over time, in particular in order to assess the consequences of the disease at its acute stage and at its chronic stage in terms of cardiovascular, pneumonological or neurological sequelae to be assessed. The assessment of this pathogenicity is crucial for our approach, depending on whether it results in extreme urgency or relative urgency. Amendment No 850 is important, and you will have a new opportunity to respond to this proposal by examining the additional article that I will propose shortly to include after Article 1. Returning to Amendment No 850, he tends to sanctify places of exercise of democracy – I am thinking above all of polling stations – by adding, after paragraph 15, the words: "Places of exercise of democracy are excluded from places whose access may be prohibited". The idea is that there might be a temptation to divert the health pass from its original use. I do not, of course, make any claims of intent to you, and I know that the sanitary pass is designed for this health crisis. Sometimes, however, a tool designed by very well-intentioned people may persist in time and allow less well-intentioned acts in another context. The temptation could thus emerge to regulate access to polling stations with the sanitary pass. In fact, today, in an increasingly pronounced context of general forbearance, the public voting today is the immunized and elderly public who largely adhere to government policy. When we see, moreover, that many young people are very abstaining and are not themselves concerned with the serious pathogenicity of covid-19, we can ask ourselves questions.

In France, the National Advisory Committee on Ethics, chaired by Jean-François Delfraissy, also president of the Scientific Council, considers, in its opinion of 9 June, that "the existing setback does not ensure the full safety of these new vaccines in adolescents". He wondered a little further whether it was "ethical to hold minors responsible, in terms of collective benefit, for the refusal of vaccination of part of the adult population".

In order to ensure equality between, on the one hand, French citizens living in France and Europe, and, on the other hand, our expatriate compatriots outside the European Union, but also in order to protect our tourism industry, we must go further in this equilibrium approach: solutions will have to be found to recognise vaccination and recovery certificates as well as negative tests obtained in third States which are in accordance with European standards.

ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u628	The main objective of this text is to do everything possible to ensure that as many people as possible avoid encountering the covid-19. In order to do this, we must find a balance, which is precisely the case in Article 4, between individual freedom and the need for the protection of as many people as possible. We must use all the means that science and the best legal framework can offer to try to protect as many people as possible. We are not opposed to solitary confinement, but we believe that there should be more flexibility in its implementation, as proposed by some amendments. The time frame and the arrangements for choosing the place of isolation should be made more flexible, since the mechanism proposed to us visibly undermines freedoms and poses the problem of the persons entitled to control – and the very feasibility of this control. Of course, we must respect health conditions, but we must also give the infected people the freedom to isolate themselves wherever they wish.
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u629	Mr Aurélien Pradié has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u630	Personally, I am opposed to forced isolation. Several months ago, the President of the Republic, in one of his televised speeches, referred to the possibility of forced isolation. In the following week, on the benches of the majority as well as on those of the opposition, among the health professionals of our country, among the sociologists, among the great names of the political philosophy of our country, among the constitutionalists, we heard many voices rising to say that this path could not be envisaged for a second, that for decades it had always been refused. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that, contrary to the idea that we are making of it, this is not the first time in this House to deal with major health crises. This has been the case in the past, and before you, minister, others have managed health crises of equal magnitude and have done so differently. On each of these occasions yes, when the major epidemics arrived, including the major AIDS epidemics, they went through the parliamentary debates here and asked questions, including the question of forced isolation. At the time, everyone opposed it. What we are talking about is having the police control of citizens whose only fault is to be ill, to grant them only two hours of exits a day and to ask them to ask the state representative for an exceptional exit permit. I'm sorry to say that if we dwell for a moment on the principles, we realize that this article is more about fighting the sick than about health security.
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u631	I have before me several identical amendments, Nos 239, 457, 542, 584, 638, 792 and 986. I call Mrs Le Pen to support Amendment No. 239.
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u632	It is intended to eliminate automatic house arrest for persons tested positive for covid. We really feel that this measure is being taken against the sick. There is a complete lack of confidence in the people affected by the covid-19, whose Government assumes that they will rush to contaminate others and that they will not have the civic isolation to avoid endangering others. It's extremely unpleasant. The use of the police to monitor people to ensure that they remain in isolation is also extremely unpleasant, and the police have something else to do in view of the level of dramatic insecurity that exists in our country. Moreover, this measure is counterproductive, because if you threaten people who are sick to isolate them ten days under police control by having permission to leave only two hours in the day, when they will feel the first symptoms, they will be careful not to go for testing! The effect will be dramatic, because they will not be isolated, they will act as if they did not have covid-19 and they will continue to have a normal life, which is exactly the opposite of what we are looking for. If we add to this that, by an inconsistency, the sanitary pass will be monitored without the associated identity check, we have exactly all the ingredients to make a bitter potion that risks aggravating the epidemic rather than controlling it.

Immunization may be the best weapon, but it's not the only one, and it needs to be strengthened with other means. We continue to make fun of the methods used in countries where the virus circulates infinitely less than in Europe, because they have put this isolation in place. We can also draw inspiration from countries less affected by the virus because they use other methods, including complete isolation. The danger does not come so much from people who are really sick, who remain nailed to bed and do not come out, as from asymptomatic patients, who are not necessarily aware of their contagiosity. The virus doesn't stay at home when the person leaves his or her home. What is important is to allow effective isolation, not sanction. As with any health product, in the event of malfeasance, hidden defects, problems related to the production or distribution of the vaccine, the responsibility of the laboratories is fully engaged. In addition, as with any other vaccine, medicine or other type of medical or surgical treatment in our country, in the event of an adverse effect that is responsible for a deterioration of the health of the person receiving the care – what is called a therapeutic hazard in law – compensation is provided by ONIAM, the National Office for the Compensation of Medical Accidents, which covers all the expenses, including those that might be related to the side effects of a treatment such as a vaccine. Everything is already provided for in the law and in the various arrangements. ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u1295	One of the difficulties we face in this context of crisis is that, depending on the institutions and even the services, the case law on the reception of families is of varying geometry, which is experienced as a profound injustice. That is why we are opposed to this amendment as a second debate.

ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u1297	There is a possible solution for health facilities, on which I have already asked the minister: antigenic auto-tests, supervised by medical personnel, which have the interest of issuing a result within five minutes and which would allow families to enter the facility safely. (Mrs Maud Petit and Mr Philippe Chalumeau applaud.)
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u1298	The Minister has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u1299	Three important clarifications in response to these interventions. First of all, Mr Jumel, Mrs Cariou's sub-amendment is included in this amendment, which obviously preserves families' access to their loved ones at the end of their lives. This is an essential point, which responds to the concern you have just expressed. Secondly, we also protect the reception of families in the event of an emergency. Finally, we preserve it in the EHPADs. With Brigitte Bourguignon, Deputy Minister for Autonomy, we have signed two important circulars to remind families and visitors of the rights of the institutions. But a hospital is a place of traffic and intense passages. Some services have developed very strict health protocols. For example, most maternity wards are prohibited for young children, who have difficulty respecting the barriers inherent in the fight against covid-19, but also the most basic hygiene rules. The health of infants must be protected. Similarly, certain resuscitation services can only be accessed on condition that they are equipped and comply with specific protocols. As a result, it is becoming increasingly less possible to visit relatives in the emergency services, as their free access has led to recurring problems of insecurity and violation of medical confidentiality. It is therefore common for health facilities to impose restrictions on visitors' access. We also consider that it would be incoherent – even ethically – not to require the health pass at the entrance to these establishments that accommodate fragile people, who need protection, while it is required to go to the restaurant, circus, theatre or cinema. [[Applause on a few benches of the LaREM group]] Of course, we take into account the specificities of each situation and the need to respond to the distress of patients by allowing contact with their loved ones. The measure contained in this amendment is the opposite of an inhumane measure since it aims to preserve life.

ParlaMint-FR_2021-07-22-E1018.u1308	In this explanation of vote at almost five o'clock in the morning, I shall begin by referring to the organisation of our debates. We can only welcome the organisation of legislative work in this Parliament... For more than a year, with my group, we keep repeating these three verbs: predict, prevent, plan. They base the various proposals that we make, whether you like it or not. As a fourth wave unfolds for the end of August, you tell us once again, minister, that "one could not guess." The famous delta variant that we have heard so much about in the last few days has been spreading in Europe since January. Only this government could imagine that it would win all countries, except France... You're making fun of us! That is why we are constantly calling on you to prevent and plan. As the delta variant invaded Europe, you announced in May the end of wearing the mask and the relaxation of sanitary protocols in restaurants. Everything was fine, everyone was invited to go for a walk and our dear restaurant friends to welcome ever more customers! And then you surprise yourself that the number of vaccinations is decreasing! When we say that the health crisis is behind us, people will obviously no longer be vaccinated. With regard to the vaccination obligation of caregivers, the facts are stubborn, Mr.Véran, and what I said earlier was absolutely not demagogy. I repeat: in March, in the first wave, you told caregivers that they were not at risk of wearing a mask when they were taking care of the patients of Covid-19. In the second wave, on the grounds that they were asymptomatic, you asked contaminated caregivers to continue to work and avoid any altruism towards their patients. In the third wave, finally, you told caregivers that they had to be over 50 years old to be vaccinated. No, it's not demagogy to remind him; No, they're not lies! It takes a lot of nerve to blame myself, in this House, for not promoting vaccination when I live under protection and am threatened because I said that the best incentive to vaccination was to get myself vaccinated! As for the obligation of transparency, we were unable to discuss it, as my amendment on the subject was considered inadmissible. You're forcing the French and caregivers to get vaccinated, but when will you force the labs to be transparent? Yet it is the only way to reduce the mistrust of our fellow citizens towards them. Have you discussed the application of the sanitary pass with the bosses of bars, restaurants, cinemas and all these places, who despair? What personnel will carry out health checks and identity checks at the entrance? The restaurants are already sorely short of waiters. How could they make new hires? What if we find out, after a quarter of an hour, that a person sitting on the terrace doesn't have a sanitary pass? Maybe she infected the people around her... You haven't even imagined this case when you claim to want to protect the French first and foremost! Finally, the virus and transport. Magnificent nonsense! The virus is dangerous in the TGV, so you need a sanitary pass, but it is not in the metro – it is known, the delta variant does not have the Navigo card – so the sanitary pass is not required there. How do you want the French to understand? You're making fun of the world! No one in life predicts forty-eight hours before what he's going to do in a day. You walk around, you walk past a movie theater or an ice cream shop, and you decide to stop. Without a test, without a sanitary pass, this is no longer possible. No pass, no ice cream! No pass, no room! [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] I can't imagine how the school year will be since you haven't installed air purifiers in schools. No pass, no class! Yes, ladies and gentlemen, to govern is to foresee.
